### MILO-B-stringPushLiteral

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `Hi`
- `A`
- `B`

**Rationale.** string.push takes an int literal without an explicit `as u8`. It used to demand `s.push(65 as u8)`: the arg was checked with no expected type, so the literal inferred i64 and then failed a u8 equality check. Vec.push always hinted its arg; string.push just didn't. The literal now coerces, and nothing else loosens — an out-of-range literal is caught by the coercion ("overflows u8"), and a real i64 value is still rejected (see tests/errors/stringPushI64.milo).

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/stringPushLiteral.milo`](../tests/fixtures/stringPushLiteral.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-stringPushLiteralGrow

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `16 aaaaaaaaaaaaaaa!`
- `27 {"name":"milo","name":"x"}!`
- `101 a`

**Rationale.** A string literal is stored with capacity 0, because its bytes are static. The first push therefore takes the grow path, and that path used to allocate a flat 16 bytes without consulting the length before memcpy'ing `len` bytes in. Any literal of 15 bytes or more was a heap-buffer-overflow on first push, in safe code with no unsafe block. Sizes below straddle the old 14/15 boundary.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/stringPushLiteralGrow.milo`](../tests/fixtures/stringPushLiteralGrow.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-stringPushStr

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `ab0ab1ab2ab3ab4`
- `len=15`
- `xyxy`
- `empty len=0`
- `head-tail`
- `src still=tail`

**Rationale.** In-place string append. `s = s + t` reallocates and recopies the whole accumulator per concat; pushStr grows amortized and copies only the addition. Covers the cases the growth path can get wrong: a cap==0 static buffer (must not be freed), self-append (source aliases the buffer being grown), and an empty addition.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/stringPushStr.milo`](../tests/fixtures/stringPushStr.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-stringSliceArg

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `[padded]`
- `[padded]`
- `[MemTotal: 8 kB]`
- `[dded]`

**Rationale.** A string slice passed straight into a `&string` param used to be miscompiled: the caller extracted the slice's *character buffer* and passed that where the callee expected the address of a %String, so the callee read a length out of the text bytes. It returned "" — silently, exit 0, no diagnostic — while binding the same slice to a `let` first worked. Safe code, no unsafe, wrong answer. The cause: `&string` and `*u8` both lower to LLVM `ptr`, and the C-string coercion keyed off the LLVM type alone, so it fired on Milo fns taking `&string` too. `readFd(buf: *u8)` still needs the character buffer — both shapes are pinned here.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/stringSliceArg.milo`](../tests/fixtures/stringSliceArg.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-stringViewReturn

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `alpha`
- `beta gamma`
- `4`
- `5`
- `17`

**Rationale.** Zero-copy text: a method hands back a `&string` view of its own buffer, no copy. `&[T]` could already do this; string could not, which left every tokenizer and scanner carrying bare (start, len) offsets with nothing tying them to the buffer. Same rules as the slice view: the call site freezes the receiver, and a view of anything but `self` is rejected (see errors/stringViewOfOtherParam.milo).

### MILO-B-structCloneOutOfContainer

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `alpha`
- `beta`
- `alpha`
- `beta`
- `1`

**Rationale.** A struct with droppable fields and no `impl Clone`, copied out of a container it does not own. The copy must be a DEEP one: a shallow header copy leaves the container's buffers aliased by a second owner, and whichever drops first frees them out from under the other. milo-self used to emit the shallow copy here (genCloneOfLoaded's Copy fallback swallowed any struct without a user Clone impl), which made examples/basics/ depgraph.milo abort inside libmalloc — the free came from arenaGet's returned Option, whose payload still pointed at the arena's own element.

That is the real assertion: the dead branch is never lowered or codegen'd, only type-checked.

### MILO-B-taskCaptureDropped

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `1`
- `dropped`
- `done`

**Rationale.** A spawned green task destroys what its closure captured, exactly once. Counting Drop runs rather than leaked bytes is what makes this exact: a byte count cannot separate "never destroyed" from "destroyed and the allocator kept the page". This printed nothing between the body's output and `done` until 2026-08-20, because reapTask freed the task struct and left the closure environment untouched. The release was written long before it could be turned on: running the drop glue here silenced milojs's async path, since the glue dropped captures a body had already moved out. Captures live in the environment's slots now and the glue skips the ones a move zeroed, so reclaiming here is safe.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/taskCaptureDropped.milo`](../tests/fixtures/taskCaptureDropped.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-taskJoinLate

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `t1`
- `t2`
- `done`

**Rationale.** Late/double join must be memory-safe. Before the done-cell moved into the Task handle, joining a task that had already completed + been reaped was a UAF write into freed heap followed by a forever-hang. Here t1 is reaped while t2.join() drives the scheduler, so t1.join() is a late join; the second t1.join() is a double join. Both must return cleanly.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/taskJoinLate.milo`](../tests/fixtures/taskJoinLate.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-tcpGreenConnectRefused

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `connect failed`
- `after`

**Rationale.** Links for Windows now (std/net carries no OpenSSL after the std/fetch split), and the successful green socket path runs clean under Wine (greenThreadEcho) — but Wine crashes emulating FD_CONNECT for a *refused* non-blocking connect (exit 9, no output), which is the exact case this fixture tests. Real Windows reports a refused connect via FD_CONNECT/SO_ERROR; test-windows is the authority, so unskip once it confirms there. green-aware connect: a refused connection from inside a task reports an error via SO_ERROR after parking on writability — it must not hang

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/tcpGreenConnectRefused.milo`](../tests/fixtures/tcpGreenConnectRefused.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-tcpIpv6

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `parsed ::1 true`
- `v4 literal rejected true`
- `server got hello over v6`

**Rationale.** Links for Windows now (std/net carries no OpenSSL after the std/fetch split), and the green socket path works — but Wine can't create an AF_INET6 socket at all (socket(23,...) → -1), so the ::1 round-trip is unverifiable under Wine. Real Windows in CI (test-windows) is the authority; unskip once that's confirmed there. IPv6 in std/net — a real ::1 round-trip. Added alongside the v4 API rather than replacing it: `ip4()` returns a u32 and `connect(ip: u32, ...)` bakes IPv4 into its signature, and a u32 cannot hold a 128-bit address. So v6 gets `ip6()` (16 raw bytes) + `connect6`/`bind6`. This needed the sockaddr seam: bind/connect take `struct sockaddr *` and read the family from its first bytes, so they cannot be declared per-family. AF_INET6 is 30 on darwin and 10 on linux — one of the few socket constants that actually differs — so it comes from the platform split.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/tcpIpv6.milo`](../tests/fixtures/tcpIpv6.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-tcpListenerPort

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `true`
- `true`

**Rationale.** Windows is skipped for the same reason as ioSocketStream and tcpIpv6: TcpListener needs the IOCP async tier that the Windows port does not have yet (docs/roadmap.md lists Windows as core language + std/io only). Without the skip this fixture just prints nothing there, which reads as a compiler fault rather than a missing tier. `bind(0)` asks the OS for an ephemeral port; without `port()` the caller had no way to learn which one it got, so every test and ephemeral server hardcoded a number and hoped. TlsListener has had this since the TLS server landed — TcpListener did not, which is how the postgres client's tests ended up pinned to a fixed port.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/tcpListenerPort.milo`](../tests/fixtures/tcpListenerPort.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-tempFieldReadDrop

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `7`
- `7`
- `temps 2`
- `literal 1`
- `bound 2`
- `indexedTemp 2`

**Rationale.** Reading a field off a value nobody owns still has to destroy that value. `makeRes().id` materialises the returned struct into a temporary, reads the field, and used to walk away: the destructor never ran, for the temporary and for a struct literal alike. Bound values were always fine, which is why nothing caught it. It leaks no memory when the struct owns heap, because the field read moves that field out and the moved value is released on its own. So `leaks` and ASan are both clean while a user destructor is silently skipped, and counting Drop runs is the only thing that sees it.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/tempFieldReadDrop.milo`](../tests/fixtures/tempFieldReadDrop.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-tempReceiverLookup

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `map-contains 1`
- `map-get 3`
- `vec-contains 20`
- `vec-indexof 0`
- `vec-len 60`
- `vec-empty 0`
- `map-len 20`
- `split-len 60`
- `entries 40`

**Rationale.** A keyed lookup needs the container's ADDRESS, and genLValue answers the literal "null" for anything that is not a place. A lookup on a TEMPORARY receiver therefore GEP'd from null, read a capacity out of low memory, and probed — and the probe only stops at an empty slot, so with a garbage capacity it never stopped. That is a HANG out of ordinary safe Milo (`parseHeaders(s).get(k)` is the shape), not a wrong answer, and it was there at every revision. The scalar reads next to it did not hang but leaked the whole container instead, because nothing owned the temporary.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/tempReceiverLookup.milo`](../tests/fixtures/tempReceiverLookup.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

- `overlong=65533`
- `surrogate encoded=65533`
- `out of bounds=65533`

**Rationale.** Allocation-free UTF-8 scanning: decodeCodepoint returns value + byte width so a lexer advances its own cursor without materializing a Vec<i32>. Malformed input must never read past the end and never return a partial codepoint — every bad case yields U+FFFD (65533) with size 1 so a scan still makes progress.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/unicodeDecode.milo`](../tests/fixtures/unicodeDecode.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-uninhabitedExhaustive

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `42`
- `42`
- `none`
- `5`
- `1`

**Rationale.** A variant whose payload cannot exist is not a case anyone has to handle. `Result<T, Never>` is what infallible generic code returns, and before this the reader had to write an `Err` arm and discharge it with `match e { }` - handling a case the checker could already prove impossible, which is a ritual rather than a check. The empty match stays legal and stays the explicit way to say "this cannot happen"; what changed is that it is no longer mandatory. Uninhabitedness is computed structurally: an enum with no variants, an enum all of whose variants are uninhabited, a struct with an uninhabited field, or a fixed array of a positive size over one.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/uninhabitedExhaustive.milo`](../tests/fixtures/uninhabitedExhaustive.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-unixSocket

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `listening true`
- `server got hello over unix`
- `long path rejected true`

**Rationale.** std/net on Windows needs Winsock init (WSAStartup) + WSAGetLastError; the TLS paths also need an OpenSSL built for the target sysroot. std/unix round-trip over an AF_UNIX socket. This is what the std/os sockaddr seam was for: the syscalls take `struct sockaddr *` and pick the family from its first bytes, so bind/connect can't be declared per-family. With the raw *SockAddr extern + typed wrappers, AF_UNIX is an ordinary consumer — this module contains no unsafe at all.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/unixSocket.milo`](../tests/fixtures/unixSocket.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-unwrapOrCopyStruct

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `7 9`
- `1 2`
- `7 9`
- `7`
- `3 4`
- `5 6`
- `7 9`
- `4 0`
- `green`
- `red`
- `green`

**Rationale.** F1 (design pass 2026-09): one Copy judgment. An all-scalar struct and a payload-free enum are Copy at `let e = d` and must be Copy at `unwrapOr`/`unwrapOrElse` too. The four unwrap sites used to call `isCopy` WITHOUT the struct/enum callbacks, so the same `Pt` that copied freely at a binding was rejected as "non-Copy Option<Pt>". The lowering loads the payload as a first-class aggregate and `select`s/`phi`s it, which LLVM accepts for any sized type, so the gate only ever needed to say what the rest of the checker already said.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/unwrapOrCopyStruct.milo`](../tests/fixtures/unwrapOrCopyStruct.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-unwrap_temp_no_leak

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `heap option 1 padded out`
- `heap option 2 padded out`
- `heap result 1 padded out`
- `1250`

**Rationale.** `opt!` / `res?` move the payload out of the enum, so an unwrapped value that is consumed in place (never bound) has no owner left to free it. This leaked one heap buffer per evaluation — `print(arena.get(h)!)` in a loop leaked forever. Correctness here is a leaks-run, not stdout; the fixture pins the shape so the codegen path keeps being exercised, and asserts nothing double-frees.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/unwrap_temp_no_leak.milo`](../tests/fixtures/unwrap_temp_no_leak.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-vecCallbackByValue

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `each: 1 2 3`
- `map: 10 20 30`
- `filter kept 2`
- `position 1`
- `retain kept 3`
- `fold 6`
- `sortBy 1 2 3 4 5`
- `sortByKey 5 4 3 2 1`

**Rationale.** A combinator hands its callback a POINTER to the element, and the checker deliberately lets the closure declare a Copy element by value instead of &T. Codegen has to load in that case. Only map did. The others passed the pointer straight through, so the closure read an ADDRESS as its value: each printed raw addresses, filter kept every element (a heap pointer is > 1), position never matched, and retain kept everything (a heap pointer is even, so `x % 2 == 0` was always true). All of it type-checked and none of it looked wrong at a glance — a filter that keeps everything reads like a filter whose predicate is loose.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/vecCallbackByValue.milo`](../tests/fixtures/vecCallbackByValue.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-vecRemoveDiscardedDrop

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `drop 1`
- `removed`
- `drop 2`

**Rationale.** `v.remove(i)` moves the element out of the buffer and hands it back. Discarding that value has to destroy it — but `VecRemove` was missing from codegen's owned-temp list while its sibling `VecPop` was present, so `v.remove(0)` as a statement dropped NOTHING: the element's destructor never ran and its heap went with it. The two spellings of "take an element out and ignore it" disagreed, which is what kept it invisible. Found by counting destructor runs (tests/dropCounting.test.ts) rather than by checking whether the program was accepted.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/vecRemoveDiscardedDrop.milo`](../tests/fixtures/vecRemoveDiscardedDrop.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `172032 100`

### MILO-B-vecStructElemStride

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `alpha:i64`
- `beta:string`
- `gamma:bool`
- `3 4`
- `delta gamma`
- `4 96`

**Rationale.** A Vec buffer must be allocated at its ELEMENT's real stride, because every index into it is a `getelementptr <elem>`. The self-hosted compiler sized these mallocs with a naive shape lookup whose named-struct fallback is 8 bytes, so cloning a Vec<Field> allocated len*8 and then strode len*sizeof(Field) across it — heap corruption that only surfaced once the compiler cloned a struct table out of a HashMap and read back a null string.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/vecStructElemStride.milo`](../tests/fixtures/vecStructElemStride.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

**Rationale.** len == 0 over a non-null pointer is defined, not an error: the closure runs and sees an empty slice. Distinguishing it from the null case is the whole reason the answer is an Option rather than a length.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/withRawEmpty.milo`](../tests/fixtures/withRawEmpty.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-withRawMutWrites

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `7`
- `5 6 7`

**Rationale.** The proof that the slice is a VIEW and not a copy: the closure writes through &mut [T], and the writes are read back through the raw pointer AFTER it returns. A copying implementation passes every other test in this directory and fails this one.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/withRawMutWrites.milo`](../tests/fixtures/withRawMutWrites.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-withRawNoFree

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `40`
- `40`
- `40`
- `freed once`

**Rationale.** The view must not own the memory. It is built with capacity 0, the same marker every non-owning slice in the language carries, so no drop, growth or extend path will free it. Three views are taken and discarded before the ONE free that the program owns; if any of them freed the buffer, the next read is a use-after-free and the explicit free is a double free. Both are what scripts/asan-sweep.ts catches.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/withRawNoFree.milo`](../tests/fixtures/withRawNoFree.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

### MILO-B-withRawNull

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `none`
- `calls=0`
- `none`

**Rationale.** A null pointer answers Option.None and the closure NEVER RUNS, proved by a captured counter that stays 0, not by the absence of a crash. A negative length takes the same path: the alternative is a slice whose len field is nonsense.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/withRawNull.milo`](../tests/fixtures/withRawNull.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

- `-2147483648`
- `256`
- `-2147483648`
- `0`

**Rationale.** @wrapping makes a whole routine's arithmetic modular (defined two's-complement wrap / masked shift) instead of trapping. Div-by-zero and bounds still trap; see the runtime-errors fixtures. Per-op .checkedAdd still opts a single op back into checking.

### MILO-B-wsFrameRoundtrip

A conforming implementation shall accept this program, and running it shall write exactly the lines below to standard output.

- `Text 1 hello ws`
- `same variant: true`
- `Binary 2 2`
- `rejected: reserved opcode`

**Rationale.** std/crypto has no Windows arm (std/ws binds OpenSSL through it), and `pipe` is POSIX. Frame encode → decode over a pipe. Locks WsOpcode's discriminant to the RFC 6455 wire nibble: sendText must put a 1 in the header, and recv must decode it back to WsOpcode.Text. A drifted discriminant would still typecheck, so only a byte-level round-trip catches it.

*Program:* [`tests/fixtures/wsFrameRoundtrip.milo`](../tests/fixtures/wsFrameRoundtrip.milo) — *verified by:* tests/run.test.ts — `fixtures (compile + run)`

- `c03356d44653358a`
